# Audio audit: the "Made for Feetza" sounds

Every sound credited as "Made for Feetza" in `public/audio/credits.json` (synthesized with ffmpeg and Python, often layered with Kenney CC0 impacts) as of the start of the Freesound audio pass: 517 files. Each has what it is (from its credit and where the code plays it), how often a player hears it, and a verdict:

- **replace**: a real recording would clearly be better (the kind is named);
- **layer**: keep the synth, add a real layer;
- **keep**: stylised (magic, sci-fi, UI, music) that a recording would not improve.

Priorities for replace and layer: **P1** heard constantly or in Acts 1–3 (smacks, thrown props, the early bosses) and clearly weak; **P2** boss moves and the mid and late acts, and hero and god smack tails; **P3** rare (hero and god peel moves, nemesis stages, sightings). **done** means it was replaced or layered in batch 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 or 6 (see below).

Sounds are built with `scripts/real-sfx/build.py` from Freesound HQ previews fetched with `scripts/sfx-fetch/freesound.mjs` (CC0 and CC-BY only), replacing the file under the same name; every source is credited in `public/audio/CREDITS.md` and `public/audio/credits.json`.

## Summary

| Verdict | Count | By priority |
|---|---|---|
| replace | 288 | P3 45, done 243 |
| layer | 83 | P3 28, done 55 |
| keep | 146 | – |

## Batch 1 (done)

The P1 sounds: 32 files, replaced or layered from real recordings. Each keeps its name, attack and place in the mix, and its length to within about 0.1 s (trailing silence is trimmed); its level sits halfway between the old file's 50 ms RMS peak and the level matching its momentary loudness (EBU R128, loudest 400 ms), within about 2 dB of the old file on both (the table below has the numbers), so the TRIM table in `src/systems/audio.ts` still holds (the five smack tails' trims in `src/systems/smackTrim.ts` were re-measured by the smack rule). The batch-1 files are skipped by `scripts/boss-sfx/integrate.py`, `scripts/throw-sfx/integrate.py` and `scripts/smack-sfx/integrate.py`, so re-running the old generators does not put the synth back.

## Batch 2 (done)

The four P1 layers left (the Head Waiter's tray toss, King Honk's pie, the Cosmic Croc's meteor and the thrown props' bonk) and every P2 sound of Acts 4–7: 35 files, by the same method and to the same standard as batch 1 (the recipes are in `scripts/real-sfx/build.py` after batch 1's). Most **layer** files keep the old synth whole, with the real recordings mixed with it (Doomstomp's getting-up growl swells only its real layers, so the old growl stays as it was); Doomstomp's stomp, though a layer, keeps only the old file's sub, and three **replace** files (Nonna's rolling pin, the Yeti's avalanche and Doomstomp's knock-out bell) keep only the old file's low end under the recordings. So four files keep just the old low end, as batch 1's big step did, which keeps them as weighty as before on both loudness measures. Each layer was rendered on its own and measured against its mix: in those four the recordings lead on momentary loudness (by about 1 LU for the rolling pin, whose rolls surge back and forth, 4 LU for the stomp and 8–10 LU for the avalanche and the bell), and the quieter real layers elsewhere are sweeteners 6–10 dB under (such as the doubled coo, the cream on the pie, the footsteps after the whistle, the bubbles under the dough). Every file sits within 2 dB of the old one on both the 50 ms RMS peak and the max momentary loudness (the table has the measured numbers), with sample peaks no higher than -1.3 dBFS (the fuse and the cash register are limited lower, at -3.1 dBFS before encoding, because their bright transients overshoot once encoded); the TRIM entries in `src/systems/audio.ts` are unchanged and their notes re-measured. None of these is a smack tail, so `src/systems/smackTrim.ts` is untouched. `scripts/boss-sfx/integrate.py`, `scripts/throw-sfx/integrate.py` and now `scripts/act7-audio/integrate.py` skip these files.

## Batch 3 (done)

Every P2 sound of Acts 8 and 9: 37 files, 29 replaced and 8 layered, by the same method and to the same standard as batches 1 and 2 (the recipes are in `scripts/real-sfx/build.py` after batch 2's). The eight **layer** files keep the old synth whole, the real recordings mixed with it (the Bifröst bed keeps its shimmer, thin wind, thunder and fire); no **replace** file in this batch keeps any of its old synth. The voices are real: the Skald is an iron-age lyre (its single plucks stacked into two strums and a rising run) and a man singing "hey", cut into a three-note phrase and a shout and pitched from -1.6 to +3 semitones onto the lyre's notes, in a short room; the Sirens are a woman's wordless sung phrase, 3 semitones up and doubled a third above, in a soft room. Sources are described as what they are (from their Freesound descriptions): the serpent hisses are a pet snake and a cat, the sea surge is an underwater recording, the Minotaur's hooves are a recording of hooves clattering (its description says no more) and his scrape is granite on granite, the Harpy's screech is a peacock (its recordist added reverb) with a red kite, and Fenrir is a howl cut from a recording of howling wolves, doubled an octave under for his size. Each layer was rendered on its own and measured against the others in its mix, and the quiet ones brought up until they can be heard: the lyre's strums sit level with the Skald's voice, and the quietest credited layers are sweeteners 6–13 LU under the loudest moment of their mix on momentary loudness (the hooves on each beat of the Minotaur's charge, the water under Charybdis's gulp, the gas burst in Muspel's scorch, the crack under the Frost Giant's shimmer, the octave under Fenrir's howl). Every one-shot sits within 2 dB of the old file on both the 50 ms RMS peak and the max momentary loudness (at most 1.6 dB off on either; the table has the measured numbers), its length within 0.1 s, with sample peaks no higher than -1.1 dBFS; the TRIM entries in `src/systems/audio.ts` are unchanged and their notes re-measured, and none is a smack tail. The two ambience loops are 50 s stereo 96 kbps beds, as before: the take runs 5 s past the loop and that tail is crossfaded (equal power) into the head, three copies are levelled and limited end to end and the middle one kept, so no filter edge touches the seam. Both sit at -16.5 LUFS integrated (the old Jotunheim bed -16.5, the old Bifröst bed -16.6) with max momentary loudness within 1.7 LU of the old, sample peaks at -2.1 and -2.3 dBFS, and their seams check clean: the jump across each seam is 0.003 and 0.009 against a 99th-percentile sample-to-sample step of 0.08 and 0.07, the level either side of the seam within 0.8 dB, and no gaps (short-term loudness never below -20.1 LUFS). The Act 8 and Act 9 integrate scripts (`scripts/act8-sfx/integrate.py`, `integrate_act8a.py`, `integrate_act8b.py` and `scripts/act9-audio/integrate9.py`) now skip these files.

## Batch 4 (done)

The last P2 sounds: Act 10's 12 and the 39 hero and god smack tails with real-world content, 51 files, 35 replaced and 16 layered, by the same method and to the same standard as batches 1–3 (the recipes are in `scripts/real-sfx/build.py` after batch 3's). The 16 **layer** files (the Sole Proprietor's entrance, Sole Proprietor Unbound's entrance and gauntlet wind-up, and 13 tails) keep the old synth whole, the real recordings mixed with it; no **replace** file keeps any of its old synth. Sources are described as what they are (from their Freesound descriptions): Batpeel's wings are an umbrella flapped like bat wings (foley) over a pigeon's wingbeats; the Symbiote Pan's slime is a rubber stress ball squished; the Swamp Thin Crust's bog is water bubbling through wet coffee grounds; Athena's shield and Poseidon's trident are a hit on a metal shed door (a piece newagesoup cut from a shed-door recording); Freyr's golden boar is a pig's growling grunt; Frigg's spinning wheel is a bicycle wheel spinning (Freesound has no CC0 or CC-BY recording of a spinning wheel for yarn); Hestia's warm bell is a Tibetan singing bowl, lower; Heimdall's horn is a battle horn (recorded with some echo), pitched down; the Sole Proprietor's whistle is a tugboat's steam whistle; the Ghost of Feet Future's rewind is a real cassette deck, its steady whirr stepped up in pitch in four overlapping cuts as the tape speeds up; and Sole Proprietor Unbound's choir is a medium-sized crowd singing "ah" on an F, pitched into a low D minor chord. Apollo and Bragi play the Skald's iron-age lyre (Bragi is the Norse god of poetry), a strum and a rising run, 7 semitones up; Aphrodite is a real harp glissando, an octave up. The smack tails stay short and on the smack: every tail keeps its length to within 0.06 s, and the first moment within 10 dB of its loudest lands within 0.05 s of the old tail's (Amazon Queen's real lasso starts 0.12 s sooner than the old swing's build, so it lands on the smack). Each layer was measured with and without it in the finished mix (the layer is the full mix minus the mix without it), and the quiet ones brought up until they can be heard (the douse's wet splat and Freyr's coin came up; a second bow under Hawk-Eye-Talian's and a heavy swing under the gauntlet wind-up were dropped): the quietest credited layers sit 8–13 dB under their mix's 50 ms RMS peak (the douse's sizzle and steam about 20 dB, as the old hiss sat well under its smack) but lead the band they fill, within 3 dB of the mix there (Freyr's coin and the Sole Proprietor's coins in the top octave after the grunt and the gong, Frigg's bicycle wheel in the low end the old whirr leaves empty, the sizzle and steam in the top octave after the smack, where nothing else plays). Every one-shot sits within 1.5 dB of the old file on both the 50 ms RMS peak and the max momentary loudness (the table has the measured numbers), its length within 0.06 s, with sample peaks no higher than -1.2 dBFS (the rewind and the frost crackle are held in by a fast compressor and limited at -3.1 dBFS before encoding, as the fuse and the cash register were in batch 2; the frost comes out at -12.8 dB on the 50 ms RMS peak, not the recipe's -12.0, because the limiter holds its cracks, and its momentary loudness matches the old file's to 0.1 LU). The TRIM entries in `src/systems/audio.ts` are unchanged and the Act 10 notes re-measured; the 39 tails' trims in `src/systems/smackTrim.ts` are re-measured by the smack rule in `scripts/smack-sfx/integrate.py` (each is still bound by the hero tails' -22 LUFS momentary target, as before, so each plays at the same momentary loudness; the trims move by at most 0.12). The First Pizzeria's ambience is a 50 s stereo 96 kbps loop as before (the take runs 5 s past the loop, crossfaded equal power into the head, levelled on three copies end to end): crickets at night in a garden, a pine-wood bonfire on a night in the mountains (crickets in it too), a fire crackling in a fireplace and a gentle breeze, its crackle's sharpest edges off and its pops held in by a gentle compressor. It sits at -16.5 LUFS integrated (the old bed -16.5) with max momentary loudness -14.6 (the old -13.9) and short-term loudness between -17.3 and -15.7 (no gaps; the old -19.5 to -14.6), sample peak -3.2 dBFS, and its seam checks clean: the jump across the seam is 0.043 against a 99th-percentile sample-to-sample step of 0.126, the level either side of it within 0.1 dB. Three Endgame variants' tails (the Incredible Bulk's, Hawk-Eye-Talian's and Wasabi Wasp's), which are their hero's tail with a synthesized portal ring on top, are rebuilt the same way on their hero's new real tail, the portal ring rendered by the old generator (`portal()` in `scripts/smack-sfx/build.py`) and set against the tail as it was in the old file; they are **keep** rows (they follow their hero's tail), within 0.6 dB of the old files on both measures (the last three rows of the table), their trims re-measured. `scripts/smack-sfx/integrate.py` and `scripts/finale-audio/integrate.py` now skip these files, so re-running the old generators doesn't put the synth back; the game boots with `?dev` and every file loads and decodes (no 404s or console errors).

## Batch 5 (done)

The first P3 sounds: the nemesis stages of the Kratos Peel in Tartarus (Kronos and Zeus, 41) and Leonidas's Hot Gates (the Persians, the Spartans, the Immortal Brute and Xerxes, 34), 75 files, 64 replaced and 11 layered, by the same method and to the same standard as batches 1–4 (the recipes are in `scripts/real-sfx/build.py` after batch 4's). They are heard only with those hero peels equipped, so each stage shares one kit: for Kronos, heavy stone (a heavy stone grinding, a heavy stone door sliding open, a small stone avalanche, a rock smashed, a crash of tiles, stone and metal on a hard floor), heavy chains (rattled, dragged and dropped) and big cats (caged lions, a tiger, a black bear); for the Persians and the Spartans, a horse at full gallop, a bow and its arrows, a battle horn, a thick metal plate for the bronze, and men's shouts. The 11 **layer** files (Kronos's landing, the clock's tick, the jaw forced open, TITAN UNCHAINED and the blade through his forcefield; Zeus's entrance, his bolt and hurled thunderbolt, his call to Poseidon and Hera's broken crown; and Xerxes's entrance) keep the old synth whole, the real recordings mixed with it; no **replace** file keeps any of its old synth. Sources are described as what they are (from their Freesound descriptions): the sand under the hourglass is gravel its recordist edited to sound like sand; the crust is a bite of crispy toast and a corn chip; the weight under the big impacts is patchen's massed stomp on a plywood floor (about eight people, comped to sound like a hundred), far lower, not the giant step, which its maker built rather than recorded; the rock cracks are a rock smashed, not the earthquake crack earlier batches used, which is mixed partly from a synthesized rumble; the bronze shields are a thick metal plate struck with a small hammer; the rhino is agitated rhinos tussling at a zoo, with a bison's nasal huff, and its hoof scrape is two pieces of granite scraped together; Ares's second horn is a note blown on a cow horn (or a conch: its recordist names both); Zeus's choir and Xerxes's is a crowd singing "ah"; the war cries are a small group of men shouting outdoors (not in English), a crowd roaring, men shouting "hey" and two men (the recordist and a friend) shouting "hey" over and over; Xerxes laughs with a man's sinister laugh, cut into a ha-ha-ha-ha-ha and a big HAAA, in a hall; the arrow storm is eight bows released and then 42 copies of an arrow loosed and flying off, staggered at other pitches; Kronos's falling boulder is a super-heavy swoosh played backwards, so it swells as it falls. Each layer was measured with and without it in the finished mix (the layer is the full mix minus the mix without it), and the quiet ones brought up until they can be heard (the chain crashing down, the crust under the chomp, the bear under the roars, the rhino's huff, the coins in Hera's crown, the choir under Zeus's fall, the hooves under the rhino's charge) or dropped (a heavy swing under Kronos's blade); a layer that is quiet against the whole file but leads a moment of its own was checked in 50 ms windows: the Brute's groan before he topples, Xerxes's backwards swoosh before his crash and the chains' first rattle before they crash down are the only sound in their first 0.3–0.45 s, and the plank under the chariot wheel's hub carries the splinters after the snap (within 6 dB of the mix from 0.15 s on). Every one-shot sits within 2 dB of the old file on both the 50 ms RMS peak and the max momentary loudness (at most 1.9 dB off on either; the table has the measured numbers), its length within 0.1 s, its attack where the old one's was (65 within 0.06 s and the rest within 0.11 s, except the hourglass flip and Kronos's death, which reach their loudest at the old files' moment but begin their rise 0.17 s sooner), with sample peaks no higher than -1.1 dBFS. Matching the old files' momentary loudness needed the real recordings shaped like the synth: the big stone hits are damped fast and then ring on lower, the darker ones (the stone head rising and sinking behind the table, the chariot's roll, the Brute's crashes) are muffled, and two (the chomp and Poseidon's wave) keep their raw mix under the master's compressor so their hits stay sharp. The scrape, Hera's crown and the arrow storm are held in by the limiter at -3.1 dBFS, as batch 4's frost was, and the arrow's thunk by the usual one (each comes out 0.3–0.8 dB under its recipe's level, and within 2 dB of the old file on both measures). The TRIM entries in `src/systems/audio.ts` are unchanged and their notes re-measured; none is a smack tail. The Hot Gates ambience is a 50 s stereo 96 kbps loop as before (the take runs 5 s past the loop, crossfaded equal power into the head, levelled on three copies end to end): howling wind with moaning gusts (a vintage sound-library recording) and stormy winds on a steep coast, each mono take a second apart in each ear, large waves crashing into large rocks (its one big breaker eased back), a hand frame drum struck far off and lower in the old bed's two passages of war drums (14 and 18 beats), and twice the battle horn far off across the water, echoing off the cliffs. It sits at -16.5 LUFS integrated (the old bed -16.6) with max momentary loudness -14.2 (the old -15.0) and short-term loudness between -18.5 and -15.1 (no gaps; the old -17.8 to -15.5), sample peak -2.7 dBFS, and its seam checks clean: the jump across the seam is 0.037 against a 99th-percentile sample-to-sample step of 0.071 (the level 0.5 s either side differs by 2.6 dB with the surf, but the 0.1 s levels run on smoothly across it). Measured in the windows where each part plays, the wind, the surf and the drums sit within 4–7 dB of the bed, the horns 8–9 dB under it, as far-off horns. All 75 rebuild byte for byte from the fetched previews, and every earlier batch rebuilds unchanged. `scripts/nemesis-audio/integrate.py`, `integrate_titanfight.py` and `integrate_hotgates.py`, `scripts/persian-sfx/integrate.py` and `scripts/god-sfx/integrate.py` now skip these files, `scripts/hero-sfx/integrate.py` leaves the three god-sfx feet sounds out altogether (it would have replaced their credits), (the nemesis integrate scripts skip all 60 of the stages' boss sounds, since a full render puts every recipe file in one report) and `scripts/nemesis-audio/ambience_hotgates.py` refuses to render into `public/audio`, so re-running the old generators doesn't put the synth back.

## Batch 6 (done)

The rest of the nemesis stages: Batpeel's Goth-Ham Rooftops (Bunion, the Jokester, the Dark Kneader and the rooftop ambience, 33), the comics villains (the Red Sole, Venomnom and the Cheetoe, 27) and the hero variants (8), 68 files, 62 replaced and 6 layered, by the same method and to the same standard as batches 1–5 (the recipes are in `scripts/real-sfx/build.py` after batch 5's), and one leftover from batch 2: the bone quake's earth crack. They are heard only with those hero peels equipped, so each stage shares one kit: for Bunion, a massed stomp, a crash of tiles, stone and metal, crunching toast, a hand sprayer's water, bottle glugs, a deflating balloon and compressed air; for the Jokester, two real jack-in-the-boxes (one cranked through "Pop Goes the Weasel", one popping open), a spring door stop, a party popper and a party horn, a joker-like laugh and real cartoon props (a small wooden slide whistle, a wind-up chattering teeth toy, a trombone's sad "wah-wah-wah-wahhh"); for the comics villains, boots, a deep evil laugh, a hose, a fire hydrant and steam, wet foley (washcloths dropped into water, a sodden sponge, a rubber stress ball, tape peeled off), a wounded man's scream and real cats (growls, hisses, yowls, an angry cat's snarl); the hero variants reuse the earlier batches' thunder, fire, horns and raven, with a woman's shout and a trumpet's fanfare note. The 6 **layer** files (the Jokester's defuse and gift box, the Red Sole's death, and the Endgame variants', Zeus, King Unbound's and the Amazon Queen's moves) keep the old synth whole, the real recordings mixed with it; no **replace** file keeps any of its old synth. Sources are described as what they are (from their Freesound descriptions): the Jokester's big blasts are a firecracker in a garage, pitched down and layered by its recordist; his flag gun's snap is a flapping sound made for wing flaps (it suits a flag, its maker says); his laughing gas's pfft is two pillows touched together; the Red Sole's alarm is a tornado siren (egomassive's edit of audible-edge's recording of a tornado siren in Streamwood, Illinois, both CC0) winding up, sped up, three times; Bunion's shove is a gloved hand's thud on a helmet; his burst tubes' spray is compressed air released from machinery on an oil rig; the Cheetoe's rustling bush is bamboo, its recordist says; Venomnom's screech is an inhaled squeal (the three laughs and the screech are their recordists' own voices, so they are called neither a man's nor a woman's); the turret's rise is an office chair's hydraulic piston. Every source was checked for "made from", "using" and "a mix of": the only composite considered, qubodup's Puff of Smoke (a mix of five other uploads), was swapped for the pillows, the tornado siren's one upstream recording is CC0, and the metal shed door under the Red Sole's cap (newagesoup's small-metal-hit-05, reused from batch 5) was cut from EpicWizard's shed-door recording, also CC0. Each layer was measured with and without it in the finished mix (the layer is the full mix minus the mix without it), and the quiet ones brought up until they can be heard (the debris under Bunion's landing and smash, the sloshing tube, the corn chip in the break, the deflating balloon, the giggling crowd, the spring's twang, the splat's cream, the 20 lb weight's clank, the turret's piston, the Red Sole's boots, halt and sirens, the short gust under the cleared gas, the sponge under Venomnom's entrance and growl, the slime under his gulp and split, the bush in the Cheetoe's flush and death, her growl, the low voice of the Ghost of Sparta's shout, the cow horn under Odin's) or dropped (bubbles under the pump, a second splat and a body fall under Bunion's death, a slap under the spring, a whoosh under the toss and the pie, the teeth's winding, an anvil under the weight, a bush under the Cheetoe's entrance, a far yowl under her death, a third claw under the scratch); a layer that is quiet against the whole file but leads a moment of its own was checked in 50 ms windows: the Red Sole's four boot steps lead 0–0.9 s before his laugh, the Jokester's fading laugh leads 0–1.3 s before his blast, Venomnom's stretching tape leads 0–0.3 s before it rips, and the Red Sole's siren whoops lead 0.3–1.25 s; the turret's piston is within 6 dB of the mix from 0 to 0.75 s. Every one-shot sits within 2 dB of the old file on both the 50 ms RMS peak and the max momentary loudness (at most 1.7 dB off on the RMS peak and 1.8 on the momentary loudness; the table has the measured numbers), its length within 0.09 s, its attack where the old one's was (58 within 0.06 s, 65 within 0.11 s; of the other three, the Endgame and Unbound moves are layers whose kept synth starts where it did, and the VENOM SURGE roar swells in over 0.13 s), with sample peaks no higher than -1.0 dBFS. Matching the old files' momentary loudness needed the real recordings shaped like the synth: twelve hits (Bunion's landing, smash, tube hit, drain, break, shove and death, the Jokester's blast, splat and the weight's thud, the Red Sole's stomp and Venomnom's gulp) go through a lighter master compressor, LIGHT (from -8 dB, 2:1), so their attacks stay sharp, and their debris and splats are damped; the dense ones (the swooshes, the dash and slash, the giggles, the growls and the shriek) are compressed in the recipe instead. Two props are sped up without changing their pitch (tempo()): the jack-in-the-box's tune by 1.53 to fit the old 1.7 s wind-up, and the slide whistle's 2.4 s fall by 1.34 to fit the old 1.8 s. The defuse, the bang, the teeth, the tick, the cap, the slash, the scratch, the dash, the pie, the break, the tongue and the Amazon Queen's cry are held in by a lower limiter (-2.5 to -4.4 dBFS) for MP3 overshoot; the ones that come out under their recipe level (by 0.2–0.8 dB) are within 2 dB of the old file on both measures. The TRIM entries in `src/systems/audio.ts` are unchanged and their notes re-measured; none is a smack tail. The Goth-Ham ambience is a 50 s stereo 96 kbps loop as before (the take runs 5 s past the loop, crossfaded equal power into the head, levelled on three copies end to end): heavy rain on a large plastic shed roof, rain running in a house's gutter, strong mountain gusts of wind far off and darkened, two distant thunders where the old bed's rolled (a boom of distant thunder at 12.5 s, and a distant thunderstorm at 35 s, both a little lower) and once, at 22 s, an ambulance's siren far off, echoing (its two-tone wail, cut from 4.0 s in the recording, where the siren sounds). It sits at -16.5 LUFS integrated (the old bed -16.6) with max momentary loudness -15.2 (the old -13.4) and short-term loudness between -17.8 and -16.0 (no gaps; the old -19.0 to -13.9: the steady roof rain is louder than the old hiss, so the thunders swell it less), sample peak -2.5 dBFS, and its seam checks clean: the jump across the seam is 0.028 against a 99th-percentile sample-to-sample step of 0.165 (the levels 0.5 s either side are -21.6 and -22.2 dB). Measured in the windows where each part plays, the roof rain sits 3 dB under the bed, the gutter 7 dB, the wind 9 dB, the thunders 2–4 dB and the siren 5 dB, as a faint far-off siren whose tones (1.85 and 2.35 kHz) stand 6–8 dB clear of the rain in their bands. The bone quake (`sfx_doomstomp_quake`, batch 2) used a designed earthquake crack, a composite of other uploads (one since deleted from Freesound, so its licence can't be checked); that layer is now a rock smashed three times and rock crumbling, both real recordings, both lower, with batch 2's window shaking and the old synth kept, within 0.2 dB of the batch-2 file on both measures (its TRIM note re-measured). All 69 rebuild byte for byte from the fetched previews, and every earlier batch's recipes are unchanged except the quake's (a batch-4 helper's name, `endgame`, is kept: batch 6's Endgame recipe is `heroendgame`). Freesound's API allows 2,000 requests a day, and the earlier batches had used them: batch 6's 63 new previews were saved from each sound's public Freesound page (its title, author, licence and description read there), politely, one page every 2 s; they are the same HQ ogg previews `scripts/sfx-fetch/freesound.mjs get` downloads. `scripts/gotham-audio/integrate.py` and `scripts/nemesis-audio/integrate.py` and `integrate_hotgates.py` now skip these files (a full nemesis render puts every recipe file in one report), and `scripts/gotham-audio/ambience.py` refuses to render into `public/audio`, so re-running the old generators doesn't put the synth back.

## Up next (batch 7 and later)

Every P1 and P2 sound is done, and batches 5 and 6 took all the nemesis stages. 73 P3 sounds are left (45 replace, 28 layer), all rare:

- **Hero and god peels' charged moves (68):** god peels (37) and hero peels (31).
- **The rest (5):** the God Peel (2), hero sightings (2) and the Last Slice's heartbeat loop in Act 10 (a layer).

Batch 7 can take the god peels' charged moves (37), then batch 8 the hero peels' (31) and the last five. The API's daily quota (2,000 requests) ran out during batch 6: a batch's searches and fetches fit in a day's quota if the info calls are kept to the candidates actually used.
